{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_1","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc \"sed -n '1,260p' /app/convert_data.py\"","aggregated_output":"\"\"\"\nConvert NWB data from the MAP dataset (Li et al.) to the decoder-compatible format.\n\nReference papers:\n- \"Brain-wide neural activity underlying memory-guided movement\" (data paper)\n- \"Brain-wide analysis reveals movement encoding structured across and within brain areas\" (method paper)\n\nProcessing decisions:\n- Align to go cue onset\n- Extract -2.5s to +1.5s relative to go cue\n- 50ms time bins for firing rates (spike counts / bin_width)\n- QC filtering: use 'good' classification from NWB (matches classifier-based QC in paper)\n- Trial filtering: exclude early lick and no-response (ignore) trials\n- Session selection: >65% correct rate on control (no photostim) non-early-lick trials,\n  and at least 50 correct lick-left and lick-right trials each\n- Sessions with 0 good units are skipped\n\"\"\"\n\nimport os\nimport sys\nimport pickle\nimport numpy as np\nimport pynwb\nfrom collections import OrderedDict\nimport argparse\nimport warnings\nwarnings.filterwarnings('ignore')\n\n# ---- Parameters ----\nBIN_WIDTH = 0.05  # 50 ms bins\nBEGIN_TIME = -2.5  # relative to go cue\nEND_TIME = 1.5    # relative to go cue\nMIN_PERF = 0.65   # >65% correct\nMIN_CORRECT_PER_SIDE = 50  # at least 50 correct per side\n\nDATA_DIR = '/app/data'\n\ndef compute_firing_rates(spike_times_by_neuron, go_cue_time, begin_time, end_time, bin_width):\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute firing rates for a single trial.\n\n    Args:\n        spike_times_by_neuron: list of arrays, spike times for each neuron (absolute times)\n        go_cue_time: float, absolute go cue time for this trial\n        begin_time: float, start of window relative to go cue\n        end_time: float, end of window relative to go cue\n        bin_width: float, bin width in seconds\n\n    Returns:\n        fr: (n_neurons, n_bins) array of firing rates\n        bin_centers: (n_bins,) array of bin centers relative to go cue\n    \"\"\"\n    n_bins = int(round((end_time - begin_time) / bin_width))\n    bin_edges = np.linspace(begin_time, end_time, n_bins + 1) + go_cue_time\n    bin_centers = (bin_edges[:-1] + bin_edges[1:]) / 2.0 - go_cue_time\n\n    n_neurons = len(spike_times_by_neuron)\n    fr = np.zeros((n_neurons, n_bins), dtype=np.float32)\n\n    for i, st in enumerate(spike_times_by_neuron):\n        if len(st) == 0:\n            continue\n        # Only consider spikes in the window\n        mask = (st >= bin_edges[0]) & (st < bin_edges[-1])\n        st_window = st[mask]\n        if len(st_window) > 0:\n            counts, _ = np.histogram(st_window, bins=bin_edges)\n            fr[i, :] = counts.astype(np.float32) / bin_width\n\n    return fr, bin_centers\n\n\ndef preload_spike_times(nwb, unit_indices):\n    \"\"\"\n    Preload all spike times for good units into memory.\n    Returns list of arrays, one per unit.\n    \"\"\"\n    return [nwb.units['spike_times'][uid] for uid in unit_indices]\n\n\ndef get_spike_times_for_trial_from_cache(all_spike_times, obs_intervals, obs_idx):\n    \"\"\"\n    Get spike times for a specific obs_interval index using preloaded data.\n    \"\"\"\n    spike_times_list = []\n    t_start, t_stop = obs_intervals[obs_idx]\n    for st in all_spike_times:\n        mask = (st >= t_start) & (st < t_stop)\n        spike_times_list.append(st[mask])\n    return spike_times_list\n\n\ndef build_obs_to_trial_map(nwb, good_indices):\n    \"\"\"\n    Build mapping from obs_intervals indices to trial table indices.\n    Returns:\n        obs_to_trial: list of trial indices, where obs_to_trial[obs_idx] = trial_idx\n    \"\"\"\n    trials = nwb.trials\n    n_trials = len(trials)\n    units = nwb.units\n\n    # Use the first good unit's obs_intervals (all units have the same)\n    obs = units['obs_intervals'][good_indices[0]]\n    n_obs = obs.shape[0]\n\n    if n_obs == n_trials:\n        # Perfect match - obs[i] corresponds to trial[i]\n        return list(range(n_trials))\n\n    # Match by start time\n    trial_starts = np.array([trials['start_time'][i] for i in range(n_trials)])\n    obs_to_trial = []\n    for i in range(n_obs):\n        diffs = np.abs(trial_starts - obs[i, 0])\n        min_idx = np.argmin(diffs)\n        if diffs[min_idx] < 0.5:  # 0.5s tolerance\n            obs_to_trial.append(int(min_idx))\n        else:\n            obs_to_trial.append(-1)  # no match\n\n    return obs_to_trial\n\n\ndef process_session(nwb_path, sample_mode=False):\n    \"\"\"\n    Process one NWB session file.\n\n    Returns None if session doesn't pass criteria, otherwise returns a dict with:\n        - neural: list of (n_neurons, n_bins) arrays per trial\n        - input: list of (n_input, n_bins) arrays per trial\n        - output: list of (n_output,) arrays per trial\n        - brain_region_labels: list of str per neuron\n        - subject_id: str\n        - session_id: str\n        - tongue_y_all: list of tongue_y values for percentile computation\n    \"\"\"\n    io = pynwb.NWBHDF5IO(nwb_path, 'r')\n    nwb = io.read()\n\n    trials = nwb.trials\n    n_trials = len(trials)\n    units = nwb.units\n    n_units = len(units)\n\n    subject_id = nwb.subject.description  # e.g. 'SC015' - mouse name from description\n    session_id = nwb.session_id if nwb.session_id else os.path.basename(nwb_path)\n\n    # ---- 1. Find good units ----\n    good_indices = []\n    for i in range(n_units):\n        if units['classification'][i] == 'good':\n            good_indices.append(i)\n\n    if len(good_indices) == 0:\n        print(f\"  Skipping {os.path.basename(nwb_path)}: 0 good units\")\n        io.close()\n        return None\n\n    # ---- 2. Build obs_intervals to trial mapping ----\n    obs_to_trial = build_obs_to_trial_map(nwb, good_indices)\n    # Set of trial indices that have neural data\n    trials_with_neural = set(t for t in obs_to_trial if t >= 0)\n    # Build reverse mapping: trial_idx -> obs_idx\n    trial_to_obs = {}\n    for obs_idx, trial_idx in enumerate(obs_to_trial):\n        if trial_idx >= 0:\n            trial_to_obs[trial_idx] = obs_idx\n\n    # ---- 3. Get behavioral events ----\n    be = nwb.acquisition['BehavioralEvents']\n    go_start_times = be.time_series['go_start_times'].timestamps[:]\n    sample_start_times = be.time_series['sample_start_times'].timestamps[:]\n\n    # ---- 4. Check session selection criteria ----\n    # Compute performance on control (no photostim) non-early-lick trials\n    # Use ALL trials for session selection (not just those with neural data)\n    control_no_early = 0\n    correct_control = 0\n    correct_left = 0\n    correct_right = 0\n\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        is_control = trials['photostim_onset'][i] == 'N/A'\n        is_no_early = trials['early_lick'][i] == 'no early'\n        is_hit = trials['outcome'][i] == 'hit'\n        instruction = trials['trial_instruction'][i]\n\n        if is_control and is_no_early:\n            control_no_early += 1\n            if is_hit:\n                correct_control += 1\n                if instruction == 'left':\n                    correct_left += 1\n                else:\n                    correct_right += 1\n\n    if control_no_early == 0:\n        print(f\"  Skipping {os.path.basename(nwb_path)}: no control non-early trials\")\n        io.close()\n        return None\n\n    perf = correct_control / control_no_early\n    if perf <= MIN_PERF or correct_left < MIN_CORRECT_PER_SIDE or correct_right < MIN_CORRECT_PER_SIDE:\n        print(f\"  Skipping {os.path.basename(nwb_path)}: perf={perf:.1%}, L={correct_left}, R={correct_right}\")\n        io.close()\n        return None\n\n    # ---- 5. Filter trials ----\n    # Per methods: \"Early lick trials and no response trials were excluded for analysis\"\n    # Also must have neural data (be in obs_intervals)\n    valid_trial_indices = []\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        # Must have neural data\n        if i not in trials_with_neural:\n            continue\n\n        outcome = trials['outcome'][i]\n        early = trials['early_lick'][i]\n\n        # Exclude early lick and ignore (no response) trials\n        if early != 'no early':\n            continue\n        if outcome == 'ignore':\n            continue\n\n        valid_trial_indices.append(i)\n\n    if len(valid_trial_indices) < 2:\n        print(f\"  Skipping {os.path.basename(nwb_path)}: <2 valid trials after filtering\")\n        io.close()\n        return None\n\n    if sample_mode and len(valid_trial_indices) > 50:\n        valid_trial_indices = valid_trial_indices[:50]\n\n    # ---- 6. Temporal Alignment\nAll neural data is aligned to go cue onset (time 0). The time window spans -2.5s to +1.5s, covering:\n- Pre-sample baseline\n- Sample epoch (tone presentation, ~1.85s before go cue)\n- Delay epoch\n- Go cue and response\n\n### 2. Firing Rate Computation\nSpike counts are histogrammed into 50ms bins and divided by bin width (0.05s) to get firing rates in Hz. This is simpler than the sliding kernel approach in the original preprocessing code but appropriate for the decoder task.\n\n### 3. obs_intervals Handling\nSome NWB files have units with `obs_intervals` that cover only a subset of trials (e.g., 160 obs_intervals for 480 trials). This occurs when recording was started/stopped within a session. The conversion builds a mapping from obs_intervals indices to trial table indices based on temporal overlap between observation windows and trial events. Only trials with neural data coverage are included.\n\n### 4. Trial Filtering\nFollowing the paper's methodology:\n- **Early lick trials**: Excluded (animals licked before go cue)\n- **No-response (ignore) trials**: Excluded (no lick within response window)\n- After filtering, `early_lick` output is always 0 and `outcome` never has value 0 (ignore). The output_values definitions still list all possible values for completeness.\n\n### 5. Session Selection Criteria\nApplied to control (non-photostimulation) trials only:\n- Performance must exceed 65%\n- At least 50 correct lick-left and 50 correct lick-right trials required\n- 69 sessions were excluded (skipped); 105 sessions passed\n\n### 6. Choice Determination\n- **Hit trials**: choice = instruction side (the instructed lick direction)\n- **Miss trials**: choice = opposite of instruction side (animal chose wrong side)\n\n### 7. Tone Onset (time_from_tone_onset)\nThe tone onset time is identified as the last `sample_start_time` before the go cue. For most sessions, this produces `time_from_tone_onset` values ranging from ~-0.6 to ~3.3 (relative to each bin center).\n\nNotable exceptions:\n- **Session 13**: One trial has unusual tone onset (~7.4s range), likely from a replay/repeated stimulus\n- **Sessions 40-104** (subject SC038 and later): Range is [-1.5, 3.9], indicating a different sample-to-go-cue timing (~1.85s vs ~0.65s earlier sessions). This reflects genuine experimental variation across subjects.\n\n### 8. Photostimulation\nBinary input (0=off, 1=on). Photostim onset times from NWB are converted from absolute times to relative-to-go-cue times. If any photostim occurs within the trial window [-2.5, 1.5], photostimulation is set to 1.\n\n### 9. Brain Region Mapping\nDetailed CCF (Common Coordinate Framework) annotations are mapped to 15 major regions:\nALM, Striatum, Thalamus, Orbital, OtherCortex, Midbrain, Olfactory, Cerebellum, Hippocampus, CorticalSubplate, Pallidum, Medulla, Pons, Hypothalamus, FiberTract.\n\n### 10. Tongue Y-Position Discretization\n- Extracted from DeepLabCut tracking (side camera, Camera0)\n- Only positions with tracking likelihood > 0.5 are used\n- Per-session discretization using 40th and 60th percentiles:\n  - 0 (low): below 40th percentile\n  - 1 (mid): between 40th and 60th percentiles\n  - 2 (high): above 60th percentile\n- Time bins without valid tracking data default to 0 (low)\n\n## Known Issues / Warnings\n\n### All-Zero Neural Data\nSome trials in sessions 40-42 (and a few others) have all-zero neural data across all neurons and time bins. This occurs because the obs_intervals for those trials overlap very little with the actual firing window, resulting in no spikes being captured. These trials are retained in the data but flagged as warnings during verification.\n\n## Decoder Training Results\n\n### Full Dataset (105 sessions, 48,356 trials)\n| Output | Balanced Accuracy | Chance |\n|--------|------------------|--------|\n| Choice | 72.4% | 50.0% |\n| Outcome | 67.9% | 33.3% |\n| Early lick | 100% (trivial) | 50.0% |\n| Tongue y-position | 71.5% | 33.3% |\n\n### Sample Dataset (5 sessions, 250 trials)\n| Output | Balanced Accuracy | Chance |\n|--------|------------------|--------|\n| Choice | 67.5% | 50.0% |\n| Outcome | 64.5% | 33.3% |\n| Early lick | 100% (trivial) | 50.0% |\n| Tongue y-position | 56.9% | 33.3% |\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

Time from tone onset (continuous, time-varying)\n        # Find the last sample_start before this go cue (the actual sample epoch)\n        t_start = trials['start_time'][trial_idx]\n        valid_samples = sample_start_times[\n            (sample_start_times >= t_start) & (sample_start_times < go_cue)\n        ]\n        if len(valid_samples) > 0:\n            tone_onset = valid_samples[-1]  # last sample start = actual tone onset\n        else:\n            tone_onset = go_cue - 1.85  # fallback: typical sample-delay duration\n\n        tone_onset_rel = tone_onset - go_cue  # relative to go cue (should be ~-1.85)\n        time_from_tone = bin_centers - tone_onset_rel  # time since tone onset at each bin\n\n        # 2. Whether photostimulation is on at every time point (binary)\n        photostim_on = np.zeros(len(bin_centers), dtype=np.float32)\n        photostim_onset_trial = trials['photostim_onset'][trial_idx]\n        if photostim_onset_trial != 'N/A':\n            onset_val = float(photostim_onset_trial)\n            dur_val = float(trials['photostim_duration'][trial_idx])\n            # photostim_onset is relative to trial start\n            photostim_start_abs = t_start + onset_val\n            photostim_stop_abs = photostim_start_abs + dur_val\n            # Convert to relative to go cue\n            ps_start_rel = photostim_start_abs - go_cue\n            ps_stop_rel = photostim_stop_abs - go_cue\n            photostim_on[(bin_centers >= ps_start_rel) & (bin_centers < ps_stop_rel)] = 1.0\n\n        input_trial = np.stack([time_from_tone.astype(np.float32), photostim_on], axis=0)  # (2, n_bins)\n\n        # ---- Outputs ----\n        # 1. Choice: left=0, right=1\n        instruction = trials['trial_instruction'][trial_idx]\n        outcome = trials['outcome'][trial_idx]\n        if outcome == 'hit':\n            choice = 0 if instruction == 'left' else 1\n        elif outcome == 'miss':\n            choice = 1 if instruction == 'left' else 0  # miss means licked wrong side\n        else:\n            choice = 0  # shouldn't happen after filtering\n\n        # 2. Outcome: ignore=0, miss=1, hit=2\n        outcome_map = {'ignore': 0, 'miss': 1, 'hit': 2}\n        outcome_val = outcome_map.get(outcome, 0)\n\n        # 3. Early lick: no=0, yes=1 (should be all 0 after filtering)\n        early_lick_val = 0 if trials['early_lick'][trial_idx] == 'no early' else 1\n\n        # 4. Tongue y-position (discretized per session) - get raw value first\n        # Get tongue y values during this trial window using vectorized operations\n        trial_window_start = go_cue + BEGIN_TIME - BIN_WIDTH\n        trial_window_end = go_cue + END_TIME + BIN_WIDTH\n        tw_mask = (tongue_times >= trial_window_start) & (tongue_times < trial_window_end)\n        tw_times = tongue_times[tw_mask]\n        tw_y = tongue_y[tw_mask]\n        tw_lh = tongue_likelihood[tw_mask]\n\n        # Filter by likelihood > 0.5\n        lh_mask = tw_lh > 0.5\n        tw_times_good = tw_times[lh_mask]\n        tw_y_good = tw_y[lh_mask]\n\n        tongue_y_bins = np.full(len(bin_centers), np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n        if len(tw_times_good) > 0:\n            # Use digitize for fast binning\n            abs_bin_edges = np.linspace(BEGIN_TIME, END_TIME, len(bin_centers) + 1) + go_cue\n            bin_assignments = np.digitize(tw_times_good, abs_bin_edges) - 1\n            for b in range(len(bin_centers)):\n                b_mask = bin_assignments == b\n                if np.any(b_mask):\n                    tongue_y_bins[b] = np.mean(tw_y_good[b_mask])\n\n        tongue_y_trial_values.append(tongue_y_bins)\n\n        output_trial = np.array([choice, outcome_val, early_lick_val], dtype=np.int64)  # (3,) - tongue added later\n\n        neural_list.append(fr)\n        input_list.append(input_trial)\n        output_list.append(output_trial)\n\n    io.close()\n\n    return {\n        'neural': neural_list,\n        'input': input_list,\n        'output': output_list,\n        'brain_region_labels': brain_region_labels,\n        'subject_id': subject_id,\n        'session_id': session_id,\n        'tongue_y_trial_values': tongue_y_trial_values,\n        'n_good_units': len(good_indices),\n        'n_valid_trials': len(valid_trial_indices),\n        'perf': perf,\n    }\n\n\ndef map_region_to_major(anno_name):\n    \"\"\"\n    Map detailed CCF annotation to major brain region.\n    Based on the reference code regions: ALM, Medulla, Midbrain, Striatum,\n    Thalamus, Pons, Cerebellum, Hypothalamus, Hippocampus, OtherCortex, etc.\n    \"\"\"\n    anno = str(anno_name).strip().lower()\n\n    # ALM: Secondary motor area (MOs) and nearby\n    if any(x in anno for x in ['secondary motor area', 'primary motor area', 'frontal pole']):\n        return 'ALM'\n\n    # Striatum\n    if any(x in anno for x in ['caudoputamen', 'striatum', 'nucleus accumbens', 'striatal']):\n        return 'Striatum'\n\n    # {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_7","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc \"sed -n '1,260p' /app/methods.txt\"","aggregated_output":"Behavior and video tracking\n\nMice performed an auditory delayed response task 21 (Figure 1A). The instruction stimuli during the sample epoch were pure tones played at one of two frequencies (3 kHz or 12 kHz). Each tone was played three times for 150 ms with 100 ms inter-tone intervals. The sample epoch was followed by a 1.2 s delay epoch. An auditory ‘Go’ cue (carrier frequency of 6 kHz with 360 Hz modulating fre-\nquency, 0.1 s duration) indicated the end of the delay epoch. Licking early during the sample/delay epoch triggered a replay of the epoch. During the response epoch (answer period: 1.5 s) mice reported the instruction by licking one of the two lick ports. Licking (consumption period: 1.5 s) the correct lick port triggered a small water reward (0.1 ~ 0.2 ul). Licking the incorrect lick port triggered a timeout (1-3 s). After mice stopped licking for 1.5 s, the trial ended, followed by a 250 ms inter-trial-interval. Early lick trials and no\nresponse trials were excluded for analysis. Overall performance was computed as the fraction of correct control trials (i.e. no photostimulation), excluding any early lick trials. We selected experimental sessions for analysis based on following criteria: overall behavioral performance (> 65%), and at least 50 correct lick left and lick right trials each. Mice performed on average 476 (Mean; range, 130-785) trials per session with 84% correct rate (range, 65-99%; STAR Methods). During the delay epoch, mice maintain a memory of their choice and plan a movement.\n\nTwo CMOS cameras (CM3-U3-13Y3M, FLIR) were used to track orofacial movements of the mouse under IR light illumination (940 nm LED). The cameras were equipped with 4-12 mm focal length lenses (12VM412ASIR, Tamron) and a pixel resolution of 71 mm. High-speed videos from a side view and a bottom view (Figure 1C) were acquired at 300 Hz using software FlyCapture (TELEDYNE FLIR). We trained DeepLabCut 39 to track the movement of tongue, jaw and nose (Figure 1I).\n\nMulti-regional electrophysiological recordings\n\nWe designed a ﬂexible manipulator system for multiple Neuropixels probe insertions (https://www.janelia.org/open-science/manipulator-system-for-multiple-neuropixels-probe-recordings). Three to four small craniotomies (diameter, 1 \u0018 1.5 mm) were made over target brain areas one day before the ﬁrst recording session. Details of recordings made with multiple Neuropixels probes in head-restrained behaving animals have been published (dx.doi.org/10.17504/protocols.io.8tphwmn). Target regions, insertions angles, probe types, etc. are summarized in Table S2. All coordinates are given with respect to Bregma (AP, anterior-posterior; ML, medial-lateral; DV, dorsal-ventral).\n\nExtracellular spikes were recorded using Neuropixels 1.0 probes, 2.0 single-shank (SS) and 2.0 multi-shank (MS) probes 3,4 (Table S2). Three to seven recordings were made from each craniotomy. Recording depth was inferred from manipulator readings (Sensapax uMp manipulators). After completion of probe insertion, brain tissue was allowed to settle for several minutes before recording. During recordings the craniotomies were immersed in saline.\n\nProbes were conﬁgured and data were visualized and streamed to disk using the open-source software package SpikeGLX (https://billkarsh.github.io/SpikeGLX/). Data from up to ﬁve simultaneously recorded probes were acquired at 30 kHz. Sync waves (0.5 s duty cycle TTL pulses) were recorded across probes and on auxiliary channels for synchronization.\n\nPhotoinhibition\n\nLight from two 473 nm lasers (Coherent OBIS LS/LX) was used to illuminate the two hemispheres of ALM. The laser power was controlled by analog outputs controlled by a Bpod (Sanworks). Photoinactivation of ALM (centered on AP 2.5 mm; ML 1.5 mm) was performed through clear-skull cap implant11 or via craniotomy by directing the laser over the skull (beam diameter, 1.8 mm at 4 sigma). Photoinhibition of the left-, right-ALM hemisphere, or both hemispheres was deployed on a subset of ~25% randomly interleaved trials 11,21 (N = 17 VGAT-ChR2-EYFP mice). To prevent the mice from distinguishing photostimulation trials from control rials using visual cues, a ‘masking ﬂash’ (1 ms pulses at 10 Hz) was delivered using 470 nm LEDs in front of the mice throughout the entire trial. For silencing ALM, we stimulated cortical GABAergic neurons in VGAT-ChR2-EYFP mice. We used 40 Hz photostimulation with a sinusoidal temporal proﬁle (5 mW average power per hemisphere) and a 100 ms linear ramping down during the laser offset to reduce rebound neuronal activity. This photo stimulus is expected to silence glutamatergic neurons in all of ALM. 37 We silenced ALM activity during the late delay epoch (last 0.5 s), including the 100 ms ramp-down. Thus, photoinhibition always ended before the ‘Go’ cue.\n\nTo analyze the effects of photoinhibition, units with at least 10 photostimulation trials were selected for analysis (Figures 5 and S5). Bilateral ALM photostimulation reduced behavior performance from 83.2% to 71.7% (N = 17 VGAT-ChR2-EYFP mice, n = 93 sessions). To exclude the effects of rebound population activity in the thalamus 16 and other subcortical regions, we used a 20-120 ms\ntime window after photo stimulation onset to measure the effect of ALM inactivation in downstream regions (Figures 5 and S5). For ALM itself, we used 500 ms (i.e. the entire inactivation period) to calculate spike rate reduction.\n\nSpike sorting and quality control\n\nWe developed a data pipeline for preprocessing, spike sorting and quality control (https://github.com/jenniferColonell/ecephys_spike_sorting). The extracellular recording traces were ﬁrst band-pass ﬁltered (Butterworth acausal ﬁlter, 250 Hz - 9 kHz). We then sorted the dataset using both Kilosort2 32 (MATLAB version, GitHub - jenniferColonell/KS20_for_preprocessed_data: Release version of KS2 for preprocessed data) and Kilosort2.5 4 (Python version, GitHub - jenniferColonell/pykilosort: [WIP] Python port of Kilosort 2). Both sorting methods gave similar results. In the analyses presented in the paper, we used the output from Kilosort2. Each output cluster was associated with a list of 15 quality metrics calculated to assess isolation and sorting quality 7 (i.e. quality control) (https://github.com/AllenInstitute/ecephys_spike_sorting/tree/master/ecephys_spike_sorting/modules/quality_metrics).\n\nThe details of the spike sorting and quality control are described in an accompanying white paper (https://doi.org/10.25378/janelia.24066108.v1). In brief, we used 15 cluster quality metrics to train classiﬁers - one for each of the major ﬁve brain areas (cortex, striatum, thalamus, midbrain and medulla). The classiﬁers were trained based on labels derived from manual curation. We curated Kilosort2 output (28 penetrations from ﬁve brain areas) using the open-source graphical user interface Phy (GitHub - cortex-lab/phy: phy: interactive visualization and manual spike sorting of large-scale ephys data). Each cluster was labeled as a ‘good’ or ‘unlabeled’ unit. Annotators were blind to location, recording condition and trial type information. Based on the annotated labels of all clusters and associated quality metrics, we trained ﬁve region-speciﬁc classiﬁers using logistic regression. Applying the trained classiﬁers to the rest of the dataset provided lists of units that were labeled as ‘good’ and were used for the analysis in this paper. We applied the cortex classiﬁer to ALM and other cortex, hippocampus, olfactory and cortical subplate units; the striatum classiﬁer to striatum and pallidum units; the thalamus classiﬁer to thalamus and hypothalamus units; the midbrain classiﬁer to midbrain and pons units; the medulla classiﬁer to medulla and cerebellum units. We calculated the rate at which the classiﬁer incorrectly labeled ’unlabeled’ units as ’good’, using 10-fold cross-validation on held-out manually labeled units (false alarm rate): 7.8%; striatum: 6.4%; thalamus: 7.3%; midbrain: 5.5%; medulla 4.3%. This method yielded 8717 good units from ALM, 7664 from striatum, 12808 from thalamus, 7495 from midbrain and 2928 from medulla, etc. Overall, the dataset consisted of 69,943 good units recorded across 173 behavioral sessions, from which 655 probe insertions were made. This corresponds to 25.9 % of clusters reported by Kilosort2. \n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

the first axis to be split\n    @param test_ratio: between 0 and 1\n    returns 2 ndarray: output_array_1 (1-test_ratio), output_array_2 (test_ratio)\n    '''\n    # np.random.seed(1)\n    n = input_array[idx_test,...]\n    output_train = input_array[idx_train,...]\n    return output_train, output_test\n\n\ndef shuffle_together(array_list):\n    '''\n    Shuffle all the ndarrays in array_list together along the first dimension. All arrays in array_list should have the same first dimension.\n    '''\n    dims = np.array([array.shape[0] for array in array_list])\n    # print('dims:', dims)\n    # output_list = []\n\n    if not np.all(dims == dims[0]):\n        print('Error: arrays do not agree on the first dimension!')\n        return\n    else:\n        idx = np.arange(dims[0])\n        np.random.shuffle(idx) # shuffle the index\n        output_list = [array[idx,...] for array in array_list]\n\n    return output_list\n\n\ndef random_split_keep_ratio(x, y, test_ratio=0.2):\n    \"\"\"\n    Randomly splitting input x according to the first dimension, keeping the ratio of different categories (binary label y) the same, and then shuffle samples.\n\n    Args:\n        x (ndarray): (n_samples, ...) input data\n x_train, y_train = shuffle_together([x_train, y_train])\n    x_test, y_test = shuffle_together([x_test, y_test])\n\n    return x_train, y_train, x_test, y_test\n\n\ndef normalize(x, axis):\n    '''\n my_r2_score(y_true, y_pred):\n    numerator = np.sum((y_pred - y_true)**2, axis=0)\n    denominator = np.sum((y_true - np.average(y_true, axis=0))**2, axis=0)\n    nonzero_denominator = denominator != In brief, we used 15 cluster quality metrics to train classiﬁers - one for each of the major ﬁve brain areas (cortex, striatum, thalamus, midbrain and medulla). The classiﬁers were trained based on labels derived from manual curation. We curated Kilosort2 output (28 penetrations from ﬁve brain areas) using the open-source graphical user interface Phy (GitHub - cortex-lab/phy: phy: interactive visualization and manual spike sorting of large-scale ephys data). Each cluster was labeled as a ‘good’ or ‘unlabeled’ unit. Annotators were blind to location, recording condition and trial type information. Based on the annotated labels of all clusters and associated quality metrics, we trained ﬁve region-speciﬁc classiﬁers using logistic regression. Applying the trained classiﬁers to the rest of the dataset provided lists of units that were labeled as ‘good’ and were used for the analysis in this paper. We applied the cortex classiﬁer to ALM and other cortex, hippocampus, olfactory and cortical subplate units; the striatum classiﬁer to striatum and pallidum units; the thalamus classiﬁer to thalamus and hypothalamus units; the midbrain classiﬁer to midbrain and pons units; the medulla classiﬁer to medulla and cerebellum units. This is to deal with cases where for e.g. (bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride is actually 43 but evaluates to 42.999999... due to numerical errors in floating-point arithmetic.\n    if np.allclose((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride, math.floor((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride)+1):\n        n_bins =  math.floor((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride)+2\n    else:\n        n_bins = math.floor((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride)+1\n    binCenters = bin_begin_time + np.arange(n_bins)*stride\n\n    binIntervals = np.vstack((binCenters-bin_width/2, binCenters+bin_width/2)).T\n\n    # Count number of spikes for each sliding bin\n    binSpikes = np.asarray([[[np.sum(np.all([trial>=binInt[0], trial<binInt[1]], axis=0)) for binInt in binIntervals] for trial in unit]\n        for unit in spikeTimes]).swapaxes(0,-1)\n\n    if rate:\n        return binCenters, binSpikes/float(bin_width) # get firing rates\n    return binCenters, binSpikes\n\n\ndef process_one_sess(data_folder, file_path_list, bw, stride, begin_time=-3.0, end_time=3.5, save_folder=os.path.join(os.pardir, 'data_processed'), qc_mode='classifier'):\n    \"\"\"\n    Process all probe files in one session.\n\n    Args:\n        data_folder (str): the folder containing all data including QC neuron ID files.\n        file_path_list: list of full file paths of all probes in this session.\n        bw (float): in sec.\n        stride (float): in sec.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
